Tier 2 part 2: H4 (causality oracle), H6 (cancellation at every lifecycle state), H7 (multi-scheduler), H8 (resource exhaustion), H9 (virtual threads), H10 (60-minute soak).
Same fuzzer as RESULTS-hunter.txt (optimus.graph.Hunter, probes/run-hunter.sh), extended. JDK 21, loom-off entity agent unless stated. Per-child logs
under the scratchpad hunter/ directory (sweep-h4/, h6/, h7/, h8/, h9/, h9r/, soak/). Raw RESULT lines at the end of this file.

================================================================ Commands

  H4 (on by default in every mode; -Dhunter.h4=false disables it, -Dhunter.h4ring=true adds the thread's recent takes to each SELF_WAIT report)
    full sweep with the oracle:          HUNTER_PAR=5 probes/run-hunter.sh sweep 1 50
    deterministic reproducers:           probes/run-hunter.sh inv i1-a0-d0-lt1          (R9 via stale stamp, as before)
                                         probes/run-hunter.sh inv u:i1-a0-d0-lt1        (NEW: R9 via an unstamped external enqueue, 4 nodes)
                                         probes/run-hunter.sh inv n2:i1-a0-d0-lt1       (R10, as before)
  H6:  HUNTER_OPTS="-Dhunter.h6=true" probes/run-hunter.sh matrix 1 30 i1-a1-d0-lt1 i2-a1-d0-lt1 i4-a1-d0-lt1 i2-a1-d1-lt1
  H7:  probes/run-hunter.sh matrix 1 10 i2-a1-d0-lt1-s2-af1 i2-a1-d0-lt1-s2-af0 i4-a1-d0-lt1-s2-af1 i4-a1-d0-lt1-s2-af0   (and 11..20 with -s3, 21..30 with -s4)
  H8:  probes/run-hunter.sh h8 deep:2000:default@-Xss256k,-Doptimus.scheduler.syncStackSafetyLimit=10000 ...   (variants listed in the H8 table)
  H9:  HUNTER_LOOM=1 probes/run-hunter.sh inv i1-a0-d0-lt0-vt n2:i1-a0-d0-lt0-vt u:i1-a0-d0-lt0-vt
       HUNTER_LOOM=1 probes/run-hunter.sh matrix 1 20 i1-a0-d0-lt0-vt i2-a0-d0-lt0-vt i4-a1-d1-lt0-vt
       (platform-thread control, same seeds: probes/run-hunter.sh matrix 1 20 i1-a0-d0-lt0 i2-a0-d0-lt0 i4-a1-d1-lt0)
  H10: HUNTER_OPTS="-Dhunter.budgetMs=2000 -Dhunter.wdMs=5000" probes/run-hunter.sh soak 1000 60 i2-a0-d1-lt1 i4-a0-d1-lt1
  cfg grammar: i{ideal}-a{asserts}-d{delay}-lt{limitThreads}[-s{K schedulers}][-af{keepTaskSchedulerAffinity}][-vt]. matrix/soak/h8 pass every
  -Dhunter.* knob of the parent to the children. Long jobs were run with HUNTER_CLASSES=<own dir> so later edits do not rebuild under them.

================================================================ What was built

Hunter.java (all existing commands unchanged: sweep, one, inv, spec):
 - HunterAgent adds two fields (NodeTask.hunterStamp, OGSchedulerContext.hunterEpochs) and seven H4 hooks: NodeTask.setCausality entry (records
   the stamp with the stamping frame's identity), every write of currentCausalityID (frame epochs), OGScheduler.tryWakeupThread after awaitedTask
   is set (hand-out), every return of findWork, OGSchedulerContext.run before setCausality (every take, including local-queue pops),
   runAndWait before _scheduler.wait (pre-block check), WorkQueue.localPush in both enqueue paths (provenance). The fullWait timer hook from H3
   also runs the pre-block check once per deadlock tick.
 - HunterH4: the causality oracle (below). HANG dumps add the post-mortem per waiter, JMX deadlock detection (monitors and ownable synchronizers,
   for R4), per-scheduler dumps (H7) and a jcmd virtual-thread dump (H9).
 - H6 mode (-Dhunter.h6=true), H7 mode (cfg -s{K}, -af{0|1}), H8 child (--h8 variant), H10 soak child and driver, matrix command, inv u: scenario,
   HUNTER_LOOM switch in run-hunter.sh (loom-on agent from the scratchpad build-loom.sh).

================================================================ H4 causality oracle

Invariant (OGScheduler.canTake): a thread at causality c takes a task stamped (T, d) only if T is itself and d >= c, "because it's caused by work
we're currently processing", or if the task has no stamp at all. Stamps are depths. The oracle tracks frame identity: every causality level of
every context gets a globally unique epoch when a frame raises currentCausalityID to it; every setCausality records (tid, d, epochs[1..d]) of the
stamping context. Per take event (path wakeup = tryWakeupThread hand-out to a waiter, findWork = findWork's return under causality > 0, run = every
run under causality > 0 before re-stamping):
  STALE_TAKE    the frame at the taker's level c is not the frame that was at level c when the task was stamped (sameDepth: d == c, the inv shape;
                popped: d > current depth). canTake accepted a stamp whose justification no longer holds.
  unstamped     the task had no causality at all (canTake: "compatible with every scheduler"). Counted, provenance recorded.
  xschedAlias   same numeric thread id, different thread: another scheduler's thread stamped it (H7).
  SELF_WAIT     before runAndWait parks (and at each 1 s timer tick): BFS from the awaited node over waitingOn and, for started hunter nodes, their
                undone kids; reaching a node suspended on this thread's own sync stack is a certain deadlock (the R9 end state). Reported as FINDING
                CAUSALITY_SELF_WAIT with the frames, each frame node's stamp, last take path, verdict and who pushed it.
  WAIT_CYCLE    timer tick only: the same across threads (T awaits a, a depends on X suspended on U's stack, U awaits b, ... back to T).

Results, final sweep (sweep 1 50, 632 child runs, same matrix as RESULTS-hunter.txt; the sweep itself reproduces the earlier one: 553 PASS,
14 PASS-SLOW, 45 R10 hangs, 13+7 R9-classified hangs):
  R9-classified hangs with the oracle firing   14/14 genuine ones (seeds 14 x10 configs, 48 x4); 13 at runAndWait entry, i.e. before the thread
                                               parked, 1 at the first 1 s timer tick. The 6 seed-48 runs classed "causality-stall+sync-stack-
                                               inversion" without SELF_WAIT are R10 starvation (poisoned work on _qqueue, SUSPENDED heuristic only):
                                               correct negatives.
  inv i1/i4 (stale stamp)                      STALE_TAKE path=wakeup (sameDepth, stamp 2/2 frame 2 vs taker frame 4) then SELF_WAIT before park.
  inv u: (unstamped)                           SELF_WAIT before park, 5/5 configurations including lt0 and VT.
  R10 hangs (45)                               SELF_WAIT 0 (correct: starvation, not a self-wait).
  False-positive check, passing runs           SELF_WAIT/WAIT_CYCLE in 0 of 567 passing sweep runs, 0/104 H6, 0/89 H7, 0/110 H9 passing runs.
  STALE_TAKE in passing runs                   18 of 567 passing runs (seeds 9, 42, 45): canTake accepted a stale stamp and nothing deadlocked. The
                                               invariant canTake claims is violated without a hang; SELF_WAIT is the predictor, STALE_TAKE is not.

New finding from the provenance (classification: R9, second entry; not R1-R8): in seed 14, the R9 end state is not entered through a stale stamp
but through an UNSTAMPED task. The frame chain at the self-wait:
    frame 2: HMIXED#38  stamp 2/1, taken by run, previously UNSTAMPED
    frame 3: HMIXED#12  stamp 2/2, taken by wakeup+run, previously UNSTAMPED  <- handed by OGScheduler.enqueue to the waiter at depth 2
    awaited path: HMIXED#12 -> HSEQ#14 -> HASYNC#20 -> HMIXED#38 (frame 2)
HMIXED#12 is a fresh node enqueued from a non-graph thread by enqueueAndTrackCompletion (no poison). canTake treats "no causality information" as
safe for every waiter, so enqueue() hands it to a thread parked at depth 2, which runs it inside an unrelated frame; its dependencies lead back
down the same stack. Deterministic 4-node reproducer: probes/run-hunter.sh inv u:<cfg> (T parks in S's frame; a non-graph thread calls
scheduler.enqueue(Z2); Z2 runAndWaits S). Hangs at ideal 1/2/4, lt0, and under virtual threads. The javadoc on OGScheduler.enqueue concedes
"if the node scheduled has sync -> async -> sync transition can lead to graph deadlock"; evaluateNodeAsync/markAsRunnableAndEnqueue poison for
exactly this reason, enqueue and enqueueAndTrackCompletion do not, and the waiter hand-out makes the hazard reachable from any external enqueue.
In H7 the same entry dominates (fresh nodes enqueued to another scheduler's global queue, taken by findWork's scanToFindWork as unstamped).
Fix direction, added to R9's: treat unstamped as untakeable for causality > 0 in enqueue()'s hand-out and in scanToFindWork (poison on external
enqueue), or check the R9 shape at hand-out time (did not help here: at hand-out the task's future dependency is not yet visible, r9AtTake = 0
everywhere; only the pre-block check sees it).

================================================================ H6 cancellation and exceptions mid-flight

Mode: per iteration one target scope and one lifecycle state, cancelled exactly when the first node of the scope reaches it: NEW (before the
clients start), ENQUEUED (right after its parent enqueues it, from the graph thread), RUNNING (leaf, cancelled from another thread while it
busy-loops 1 ms), RUNNING_INGRAPH (a non-leaf cancels its own scope and calls waitForCancelledNodesToStop from inside run()), SYNC_WAIT (the
waiting thread cancels as it enters runAndWait on a kid), SYNC_WAIT_DELAYED (another thread cancels 200-400 us later), ON_CHILD_COMPLETED (inside
the callback, possibly on a non-graph thread), COMPLETED (after the clients are done). waitForCancelledNodesToStop is then called from a non-graph
thread with a 2 s bound. Sync parents rethrow a kid's exception (as get() would). Seeds without scopes get 1-3 (separate RNG stream). Oracles:
every enqueued node done (after a 3 s grace for orphans: a parent that rethrows leaves later kids enqueued but unawaited), no completes > 1, no
"Cannot complete node twice", no unexpected GraphInInvalidState (MandatedExceptionLogger lines counted against the expected guard throws),
waitForCancelledNodesToStop returns early only if the cancelled leaf finished.

120 runs (30 seeds x i1/i2/i4 asserts on, plus i2 with delays): 97 PASS, 7 PASS-SLOW, 7 ORACLE, 9 HANG.
Trigger coverage over the 111 non-hung runs: NEW 2379, ENQUEUED 2338, RUNNING 2076, RUNNING_INGRAPH 1243, SYNC_WAIT 643, SYNC_WAIT_DELAYED 643,
ON_CHILD_COMPLETED 997, COMPLETED 2362 firings. waitForCancelledNodesToStop from outside the graph: 0 timeouts, max 9 ms, except one
SYNC_WAIT_DELAYED wait of 1001 ms (returned within the bound; the cancelled node's wait was resolved by the 1 s deadlock timer). Never returned
while the cancelled leaf was still running (wfcnsEarly 0). Sync waiters in a cancelled scope: 618 ended with an exception, 668 with a value
(their awaited kid had already started, so cancellation did not reach it), 0 not done; every waiter whose awaited kid was cancelled got the
exception (500 cases; H6_SYNC_SWALLOWED 0). No double completion, no "Cannot complete node twice", no unexpected GraphInInvalidState in any run.
The OPTIMUS-43488 guard fired as documented 15 times (in-graph wait with a cancelled ancestor on the caller's own stack -> GraphInInvalidState).

Failures, classified:
 - 9 hangs: seed 14 x4 (R9, SELF_WAIT), seed 23 x4 (R10), seed 25 i4 x1: NEW, below.
 - 7 ORACLE (NOT_DONE + H5), all at ideal=1, seeds 12, 13, 15, 16, 19, 28, 30: NEW trigger of R10's second half (b). H5 after quiescence:
     workers=0 waiters=0 idle=0..1 qqRun/Tot=4/4 tracked=4 badState=true      (seed 13; schedulerInBadState() itself reports it)
   Poisoned evaluateNodeAsync work (or a re-enqueued SEQ) lands on _qqueue while the only slot is held by a client, so addWorkerIfNeeded refuses
   (limitThreads, activeCount >= ideal). The client then finishes early (its sync ancestor rethrew a cancellation) and leaves the graph;
   exitGraph only calls addWorkerIfNeeded when _workers.count > 0, and the workers.count == 0 branch only cleans up done tasks. Runnable work is
   left on the global queue with zero threads in the graph and an idle worker parked: it runs only when something else enqueues. Same root cause
   as R10 (b) ("exitGraph never re-checks the global queue"), but with no waiter at all: the work is stranded, not starved. Reproduce:
   HUNTER_OPTS=-Dhunter.h6=true probes/run-hunter.sh one 13 i1-a1-d0-lt1 0 3.
 - seed 25 i4 (2/12 on rerun), NEW, M: waitForCancelledNodesToStop called inside the graph spins forever. Dump (H6 IN-GRAPH-WFCNS-STUCK):
     hunter-client-2 current=HSYNC#4 (cancelled scope) is in waitForCancelledNodeToStop (Thread.yield loop, OGScheduler.java:1702)
     hunter-client-1 current=HSYNC#1 (cancelled) awaited=HSYNC#4, sync frame 1 = HSYNC#1
     hunter-client-0 awaited=HSYNC#1
   When the caller snapshotted the stacks, HSYNC#1 was RUNNING on client-1, so it was captured with frame = client-1's OGSchedulerContext, not a
   SyncFrame. HSYNC#1 then entered runAndWait(HSYNC#4), i.e. waits on the caller. The loop condition (owner.getCurrentNodeTask() == HSYNC#1) stays
   true for ever, and the OPTIMUS-43488 deadlock check only runs `if (frame instanceof SyncFrame)`, so it never fires. Permanent, one core spinning
   (R5's spin). When the snapshot happens after client-1 is already suspended (frame = SyncFrame) the guard throws as designed (the other runs).
   Fix: apply the forward-chain check whatever the frame type (it is exactly the case where the node moved from running to sync-suspended), or
   re-snapshot each iteration. Reproduce: HUNTER_OPTS=-Dhunter.h6=true probes/run-hunter.sh one 25 i4-a1-d0-lt1 0 12 (log shows
   "H6 IN-GRAPH-WFCNS-STUCK" after 2 s).

================================================================ H7 multi-scheduler

K = 2 (seeds 1-10), 3 (11-20), 4 (21-30) OGSchedulers (default + new OGScheduler("h7sN-", ideal, NORM_PRIORITY)); each node's scenario stack
names one (withScheduler(weakView)); clients enter scheduler client % K (EvaluationContext.initialize(sched, ScenarioState.minimal)); half of the
parent->child enqueues whose child names another scheduler go to that scheduler's global enqueue; promise completions and external launches use
the node's scheduler; continueWith crosses freely. 120 runs: i2/i4 x affinity on/off, asserts on.

  cfg (all seeds)   PASS  PASS-SLOW  HANG   xschedAlias takes (runs)   re-runs moved to another scheduler
  s2 af0              13      2        5      1667 (15/20)              39173 of 117229
  s2 af1              14      0        6       178 (13/20)               1963 of 145491
  s3 af0              13      2        5      1023 (12/20)              17859 of  42682
  s3 af1              13      2        5        57 (10/20)                417 of  38959
  s4 af0              13      2        5      1838 (13/20)              58346 of 108670
  s4 af1              13      2        5        78 ( 9/20)               2543 of  78758
 - Affinity oracle: with keepTaskSchedulerAffinity on, every run happened on the scheduler its scenario stack named (ssMismatch 0 in all 60
   runs; trivially, since OGSchedulerContext.run rewrites the stack before running). Affinity cuts re-runs on another scheduler by 20-40x but not
   to zero: a node sync-awaited from another scheduler's thread is run there directly (drainQueue / findWorkOnForwardChain) and re-homed.
 - 31 hangs, 21 of them on seeds/configs that pass single-scheduler. 20 carry CAUSALITY_SELF_WAIT/WAIT_CYCLE (R9; provenance as in H4: fresh nodes
   enqueued to another scheduler's global queue and taken unstamped by scanToFindWork inside an unrelated frame). Of the other 11: 6 are R10 on
   the default scheduler (causality-stall), 4 are "other:waiters-nothing-runnable" because the classifier only inspects the default scheduler's
   queues (checked for seed 6: poisoned -1/1 HMIXED#8 runnable on s1 next to s1's waiter at causality > 0, i.e. R10 on a secondary scheduler),
   1 (seed 24 s4 af0) is classed sync-stack-inversion by the heuristic with no oracle hit: unexplained.
 - NEW, L (latent, R1-like, no hang attributed to it yet): thread ids are per scheduler (each OGScheduler has its own threadCounter starting at 1,
   so the first client of every scheduler is tid 1), but causality stamps travel with nodes across schedulers. canTake then accepts another
   scheduler's thread's stamp as "own": the oracle counted xschedAlias takes in 72 of 120 runs (up to 278 per run), lower with affinity on. Same
   consequence as R1 aliasing (a thread takes work stamped under someone else's sync frame) without any overflow. Fix with R1's: a process-wide
   thread id (one static counter) or a scheduler id in the stamp.
 - R4 (cross-scheduler lock order): not reached. No JMX-detected deadlock, no "_lock held" dump, in any H7 hang. The fuzzer has no XSFT proxies
   and no cycle recovery, so tryToRecoverFromCycle never runs under _visitLock; R4 still needs the T4 fixture.

================================================================ H8 resource exhaustion

  variant                                               result
  deep:2000:default (default -Xss)                      completes, depth 2000, causality 1999 (Default NodeTaskInfo bumps every frame)
  deep:2000:amow (AT_MOST_ONE_WAITER)                   completes, causality 1900 (no bump for the first 100 frames: syncStackSafetyLimit)
  deep:2000:{default,amow} -Xss256k, safetyLimit=10000  StackOverflowError at depth ~241 in all three -Xss256k variants (amow with limit 10000:
  deep:2000:amow -Xss256k (limit 100)                   causality 0 throughout; with limit 100: bumped from frame 101, 142 at depth 241).
                                                        The bump does not reduce stack use (it only restricts which work the frame may take),
                                                        so "the causality bump instead of StackOverflowError" does not happen. What does happen:
                                                        runAndWait's catch(Throwable) logs "Caught an error in runAndWait" through logback; at
                                                        that depth logback's ThrowableProxy <clinit> itself overflows, so the class is marked
                                                        failed and every later log call with a throwable in this JVM throws
                                                        NoClassDefFoundError. That error, not the SOE, propagates out of all 241 frames to the
                                                        client; all 2000 nodes are left not done (none completed with an exception). Without a
                                                        warm-up chain the SOE also hit class loading inside the agents' transformers and left
                                                        workers=2 / spinningThreadID=3 in the scheduler (H5 badState). deep:200 at -Xss256k completes.
                                                        Classification: F1 family (Errors on the kill/logging path), NEW detail: one SOE in the
                                                        scheduler permanently breaks logback's throwable logging for the process.
  fanchain:5000 proxyChainStackLimit=2 (+/- -Xss256k,   completes in ~30 ms; 1666 delayOnChildCompleted hand-offs (every 3rd level); a
     +/- completion from a non-graph thread)            DelayOnChildCompletedNode was seen queued at most 1 at a time (sampler every 200 us); no stall.
  fanchain:5000 default limit 100, -Xss256k             completes; 49 hand-offs.
  fanchain:5000:guard limit -1 (mechanism disabled,     HANG (both). 5000-deep onChildCompleted recursion overflows; notifyWaiters' failure goes to
     as DelayedProxyNodeNoReEnqueue does), +/- -Xss256k logAndDie, which logs "logAndDie called recursively!" and returns (F1b): 1532..4733 nodes never
                                                        complete, client parked for good. Without the guard (-1 passed straight to
                                                        delayOnChildCompleted) every completion is re-delayed for ever (1.3 G hand-offs in 60 s):
                                                        harness misuse, the real proxies guard -1.
  seqchain:5000 (default re-enqueue), +/- limit 2       completes, no hand-offs (re-enqueue is iterative).
  heap:1000000 ideal=1/2/4 (-Xmx2g)                     F8 quantified: 1,000,000 completed priority-1 nodes retained on _queue while the only graph thread is
                                                        still in the graph (112 MB of node shells here). On exit: ideal=1 purged to 0 at exitGraph; ideal=2
                                                        and 4 still 997,560 / 996,204 right after the client left (the helper was still in _workers, so the
                                                        workers.count == 0 purge did not run), 0 after the helper went idle (+3 s). No leak after quiescence.

================================================================ H9 virtual threads

Build: the scratchpad build-loom.sh compiles entityagent with src/main/loom-on (only difference: LoomDefaults.enabled = true, feeding
useVirtualThreads, loomTransformations, lCompilerLevel) into out-loom/entityagent and jars/entityagent-loom.jar. vt_helpers.sc is indeed missing
from the export: InstrumentationCmds.readResource asserts it is non-null (with -ea: AssertionError in premain, JVM aborts; without -ea: NPE caught,
no config applied at all). A one-line comment-only stub vt_helpers.sc makes the agent load with and without -ea. Caveats: "Cannot patch core
classes" (no entityagent-ext jar), so InstrumentationInjector is off and no .sc directive could be applied to JDK classes even with the real file;
synchronized pins carriers as on stock JDK 21; LoomInjector only rewrites @loom classes (none here). So this exercises the scheduler's VT paths
(virtual-thread-per-task executor, no idle pool, limitThreads=false), not the full loom-on deployment. Verified at runtime: workers are unnamed
virtual threads, limitThreads=false.

  scenario                         VT (loom-on, useVirtualThreads, lt0)        platform threads
  inv (R9 stale stamp)             i1 4/4 hang, i4 4/6 hang, i4 asserts 1/1     lt1: i1 5/5, i4 3/5 (RESULTS-hunter.txt)
  inv u: (R9 unstamped)            i1 1/1, i4 3/3 hang                          hang at every ideal, lt0 and lt1
  n2 (R10)                         0/5 hang (i1 x4, i4 x1)                      lt1 i1 5/5 hang; passes with lt0
  20 seeds x 3 cfgs                60 runs: 54 PASS, 4 R9 (seed 14 x3, 15 i1),  same 60 with platform lt0: 55 PASS, 1 PASS-SLOW, 4 R9 (the same 4)
                                   1 R10-like (seed 6 i1), 1 NOT_DONE (seed 20)
 - As predicted: R9 occurs unchanged under VT (same seeds and configs as platform lt0, SELF_WAIT on every one), and R10's starvation path (N2,
   the poisoned-work seeds) does not.
 - NEW under VT only, 1 of 6 runs of seed 6 i1-a0-d0-lt0-vt: HANG with runnable UNSTAMPED work (HMIXED#11, 0/0, twice on _qqueue), 3 waiters at
   causality 1-2, workers=0, spin=0 and no virtual thread alive in the jcmd dump. With limitThreads=false waiters do not scanToFindWork (only the
   limitThreads branch does), and under VT there is no idle pool, so the design relies on a spinner existing; here the last spinner left without
   the work being taken. R10 family (lost helper), a counterexample to "R10 cannot occur under VT" in its strong form; 0/5 on rerun, 0/6
   platform lt0. Reproduce: HUNTER_LOOM=1 probes/run-hunter.sh one 6 i1-a0-d0-lt0-vt 0 10 (rare).
 - seed 20 i4-a1-d1-lt0-vt: FINDING NOT_DONE HASYNC#9 (cancelled scope) state SRiq, pending=0, its only kid notified, i.e. the count-down reached
   zero and the node is not complete at check time. Same node and shape as the open seed-20 NOT_DONE of the first platform sweep. Now seen twice
   in ~1,000 runs; still open (a complete() in flight versus the end-of-iteration check cannot be excluded).
 - No GraphInInvalidState, double completion or H5 violation specific to VT. Throughput comparable (iterations within 6%).

================================================================ H10 soak (60 minutes)

Design: one long-lived child JVM per configuration (i2-a0-d1-lt1 and i4-a0-d1-lt1: delays on, limitThreads true, asserts off), fresh seeds from
1000 upwards, 2 s per seed, a new set of client threads for every seed (a request pool that churns threads, so threadCounter advances
organically), threadCounter and _top never primed. Fuzzer oracles after every iteration (values, exactly-once, H5 accounting, H4). On a hang the
child dumps and classifies, then injects one causality-0 worker above the ideal count (the R10 fix direction) and waits 5 s: if the clients finish
the seed is HANG-RESCUED, otherwise the clients are abandoned (HANG) and the soak continues with the next seed; after more than 4 abandoned
clients the JVM is restarted at the next seed (segment). SOAKSTAT every 30 s (heap after System.gc(), live og.workers, onStalledCallbacks,
_queue, tracked nodes, threadCounter, max WorkQueue._top over all known contexts).

Totals (2145 seed results, 21 restarts / 23 segments): i2: 535 pass (9 slow), 76 hang-rescued, 47 hung (abandoned), 295 H5; i4: 671 pass (14
slow), 53 hang-rescued, 26 hung, 313 H5. 23 CAUSALITY_SELF_WAIT (R9) findings, each before the thread parked. No wrong value, no double
completion, no exception other than the expected ones, no lost wakeup (timer-branch oracle), no GraphInInvalidState (asserts off here).

Per 10-minute window (wall clock), counts:
  SOAK-WINDOW cfg=i2-a0-d1-lt1 minutes=0-10 pass=182 hang=14 hangRescued=25 other=3 classes={H5{H5}=3, HANG-RESCUED:causality-stall=25, HANG:causality-stall=13, HANG:causality-stall+sync-stack-inversion=1, PASS=178, PASS-SLOW=4}

  SOAK-WINDOW cfg=i4-a0-d1-lt1 minutes=0-10 pass=205 hang=10 hangRescued=19 other=7 classes={H5{H5}=7, HANG-RESCUED:causality-stall=18, HANG-RESCUED:causality-stall{CAUSALITY_SELF_WAIT,H5}=1, HANG:causality-stall=7, HANG:causality-stall+sync-stack-inversion=1, HANG:causality-stall+sync-stack-inversion{CAUSALITY_SELF_WAIT}=1, HANG:other:waiters-nothing-runnable=1, PASS=200, PASS-SLOW=5}

"other" is almost entirely H5, and those H5 are the harness's own doing: after an abandoned (R9-hung) seed, its clients stay in _waiters for the
rest of the segment, so every later iteration reports waiters>0, trackedNodes>0 and liveOg>idle+workers (486 of 624 H5 findings carry exactly
that signature; the rest add the stranded runnable work of the hung seed). The 40-60 minute windows are dominated by two long segments that
carried 3-4 abandoned waiters each (i2 seg 11: 857 s, i4 seg 7: 1022 s), which also starve later seeds (fewer passes). Rescue by one injected
causality-0 worker recovered 129 of 202 R10-class hangs; the rest recur after the injected spinner leaves (more poisoned work arrives later in the
same seed): one-shot injection is not a fix, a standing rule is needed.

Reading: no drift. Heap after GC stays at 14-19 MB in every window and within every segment (first->last sample differs by <= 3 MB, both
directions); live og.workers <= 5 (ideal + the rescue injections), onStalledCallbacks 0 and _queue 0 at every sample, live JVM threads 16-26.
The two counters that matter for the wrap hazards move linearly and slowly: threadCounter grew ~1-1.7 ids/s with per-seed thread churn (max 806
in one 470 s segment), so R1's 32,768 would be reached after ~5-9 h of this churn in one process (minutes if every request used a fresh thread;
H10 used one set per seed); the hottest WorkQueue._top grew ~415/s (max 195,700), so R2's 2^31 is ~60 days of this load per queue. Neither was
reached organically in 60 minutes (the restarts reset both), as expected.

New failure classes in the soak: none outside R9/R10. Two hangs classed "fenced-local-work" (runnable work only on local queues behind sync-frame
markers, one with SELF_WAIT) occurred only in segments already degraded by abandoned clients; not investigated further.

                    by any waiter; external enqueue hands a fresh node to a thread parked at depth d, which
                    runs it inside an unrelated frame; its dependency is suspended lower on the same stack.
